# ROADMAP
LinkedSpec is being positioned as a progressive extraction parser DSL: fast, recursive, regex-anchored, and intentionally different from strict EBNF-centric tooling.

Execution-oriented companion: `ROADMAP_V2.md` keeps the same live tracker and policy contracts in a shorter operational form. Keep both roadmap files aligned when a slice changes active status or execution policy.

Expressive `.spec` self-containment and an optional EBNF-like authoring profile are an accepted long-horizon
direction under ADR `0064` and `SPEC-LANGUAGE-SELF-CONTAINMENT`. Self-containment means portable closure for
grammar, recursion, cursor/capture/state, callable/control, transformation, and result objectives—not ambient
filesystem/process/network/host authority. The profile must lower losslessly into the same canonical AST/HandlerIR
and runtime as ordinary syntax, retain authored source maps/diagnostics, state semantic differences honestly, and
roll out across Perl/Rust/Dart/Julia/Lua. Behavior-free `.0` routes the full program; `.1+` is unscheduled behind
current callable-codeblock parity.

trace, and temporary workspace must reside on the repository filesystem; durable paths remain root-relative and
runtime storage roots derive from the current checkout. Cross-volume reads are forbidden by default and retained
only for explicit caller inputs or documented, strictly required external tools and operating-system resources.

## Scope and Objective
- Make LinkedSpec a serious, stable, respected parser prototyping tool.
- Preserve the current strengths:
  - Recursive/nested construct parsing.
  - Coarse-to-fine staged parsing (multi-pass).
  - Fast AST prototyping through concise rule/action syntax.
- Improve reliability, diagnostics, and maintainability without breaking existing users.

## Current Baseline (Observed)
- Core public facade: `perl/LinkedSpec.pm`; active compile/runtime ownership lives in the
  `perl/LinkedSpec/*` owner modules.
- Regex dispatch helper: `perl/LinkedRE.pm`.
- Parser loading path: `LinkedSpec::get_parser(...)` + `PathSearch`.
- Existing shipped parser/spec surfaces include `LibReader`, `PPlugin`, `TableGrep`, and the
  other checked-in `specs/*.spec` files. Downstream consumer compatibility outside the core
  checkout remains deferred unless explicitly resumed later.
- Legacy non-core/plugin code is no longer in the active core tree: `RTLUtils`, `FSMGen`,
  `VHDL::ConstantEval`, and six exclusively dependent `.plg` files were deleted under
  `LEGACY-VHDL-RETIRE`; the remaining 13 `.plg` files and their domain owners were relocated
  under `noncore/` by `NONCORE-QUARANTINE`. The root `plugin/` directory is gone, and `perl/`
  is core-only apart from the legacy compatibility adapter `PPlugin.pm`.
- Specs baseline:
  - All 21 shipped files in `specs/*.spec` compile in the phase0 baseline.
  - Current phase0 reaches `PASS 1..1029` when run with `PERL5LIB=` cleared.
  - The Rust interpreter oracle is manifest-backed at 105 fixtures.
  - The former `specs/tclite.spec` literal `[` regex blocker is fixed.

## Strategic Principles
1. Keep staged extraction as a first-class concept.
   - Current staged linked parsing status (2026-07-09):
     the first function-body prototype is proven end to end. Top-level
     user-function definition shells are parsed by `specs/user_function_definition.spec`;
     their exact body text is preserved in `body_payload`, the neutral
     `body_parse_job` dispatches through the minimal built-in
     `actionir-body.spec` / `action_block` registry provider, and the returned
     body `action_block` AST is stitched into `body_ast` on Perl, Rust, and
     the Dart staged-dispatch API.
     Exact scalar-assignment `parse_job(...)` authoring, caller-frozen logical registry entries, multiple
     payload parser families, breadth-first recursive staged queues, and cycle diagnostics are now current across
     Perl, Rust, Dart, Julia, PUC Lua, and LuaJIT. Authored identities still grant no path/provider/loading
     authority. Behavior-free audit `.14.7.0` froze their dependency
     plan after proving the exact narrow boundary across all five source backends/six
     runtimes. Corrective `.14.7.1` now closes the compile-diagnostic context mismatch:
     Perl, Rust, and Dart pass the normalized job through compile, while Julia/shared Lua
     add the missing payload-kind field to their already job-aware formatter. Neutral `.14.7.2`
     now makes the complete general design executable without backend behavior: authored
     `parse_job(...)`, typed provenance, pre-resolved immutable registry/cache authority,
     breadth-first recursive scheduling, policies/bounds/detachment, carriers, diagnostics,
     backend routes, rollout, and exact ownership pass 84 reason-checked mutations after Rust admission. Perl dormant boundary
     `.14.7.3.0` now freezes 60 GREEN assertions plus one missing-`STAGED_PARSE_JOB_MARKER` RED while leaving the
     function-body-v1 adapter, production behavior, discovery, rollout, and public surfaces unchanged. Private
     annotation/provenance `.14.7.3.1`, current-depth resolution/cache/policies `.2`, and recursive queue/bounds/
     rebased diagnostics `.3` are private foundations. Carrier/admission `.4` now proves native, normalized-
     descriptor, generated-plan, and independently loaded emitted routes under fresh host authority, makes the
     oracle 143/143, and admits only Perl once through phase-0/canonical topology. Rust `.14.7.4.0-.4` now adds an
     inert marker/provenance carrier, pure caller-frozen resolution/cache and policies, breadth-first recurrence,
     exact cycle/decrease guards, shared cancellation/resources, callback safe points, and direct/ordered-derived
     source rebasing. `.4` attaches a host-only seed to native, reconstructed, validated generated-plan, and
     independently compiled emitted routes, proves fresh cache/callback/cancellation/clock authority, removes the
     dormant cfg/dead-code scaffolding, and admits only Rust through ordinary/canonical topology;
     later backends, recurring proof, public authoring, generated format, and outward behavior remain pending or
     unchanged. Mandatory segment `4991` remains
     finitely authorized by ADR `0089` at
     22 change-history files / 21 manifest lines. Director clarification `.14.0` now distinguishes active
     in-parse progressive composition from later returned-AST staged enrichment.
     Director-approved ADR `0056` additionally makes source identity, immutable
     Unicode-scalar positions/spans, bounded effect-safe cursor transactions,
     recursive boundary provenance, and span-native dispatch one architecture;
     `.14.1-.14.8` own its contract through public no-drift.
2. Keep recursion ergonomics simple.
   - Typical `.spec` authoring uses zero/one/two small readable regexes for coordination,
     leaves, or entry/exit boundaries. Deep recursion belongs in action-edge OR and
     blind-call AND rule connections, not recursive regexes. Recursive entry/match/
     accepted-exit locations are future immutable observations, never cursor-policy
     overrides. `.14.1-.14.8` own the neutral contract, backend admissions, examples,
     and contrary-walkthrough reconciliation.
3. Make parser behavior explicit (not accidental).
4. Improve trust with deterministic diagnostics and regression tests.
5. Maintain backward compatibility by default while introducing stricter optional modes.
6. Drive `.spec` toward language-agnostic action semantics (no embedded Perl code-block dependency in final state).
   - The active terse-format direction is tracked under `SPEC-FORMAT-TERSE` and ADR `0007`:
     current `.spec` authoring prefers auto-existing variables, bare reads in value positions,
     direct shape literals, `set(...)`/operators, method-style helpers, colon hash literals,
     and the currently implemented immediate `with(...) { ... }` / `.with() { ... }` trailing block arguments over older
     declaration-heavy or Perl-shaped forms.
   - ADR `0031` / `FUTURE-PARITY-BACKLOG.11.1` now settle the corrective design: callable codeblocks use
     `{|args| body }` (`{|| body }` for zero params), `cb(args)` invocation, and dynamic caller context without
     lexical capture. Harrays and eager `{ statements }` blocks remain distinct; `with` stays an ordinary helper.
     Neutral executable contract `.11.2` is adopted and canonically checked; Perl typed-literal construction
     `.11.3.1` and dynamic variable invocation `.11.3.2` are complete. ADR 0032 declaration `.11.3.3.1` and
     metadata-governed Perl attached/parenthesized normalization `.11.3.3.2` and closeout `.11.3.4` are complete.
     Rust typed construction/state `.11.4.1` is complete: exact brace disambiguation, the neutral eight-field
     fixed/rest record, Unicode character-coordinate spans, inert runtime/semantic descriptor state, compiled JSON,
     generated-plan reconstruction, and emitted-source preservation create no host closure and execute no body.
     Rust dynamic invocation `.11.4.2` now matches the neutral caller-context model: static precedence, once-only
     ordered arguments, copied temporary fixed/rest bindings with restoration, caller-visible nonparameter stores,
     local return/final results, chaining/discard, exact typed failures/ordered recursion, and identical native,
     reconstructed, generated-plan, and emitted-source execution. Focused, complete Rust operational, canonical,
     Knowledge Map, mdBook, doctrine, and cleanup signoff is green. Generic contextual equivalence `.11.4.3` is
     also signoff-complete: attached and parenthesized blocks normalize through one callable-metadata registry,
     typed final-parameter kinds survive staged parsing/descriptors, helper/user/receiver/tree callbacks share the
     existing dynamic executor, and eager blocks plus established controls retain their prior semantics. Rust
     parent `.11.4` is closed. Dart construction `.11.5.1` now preserves the same exact inert eight-field literal,
     fixed/rest signature, typed body, containing Unicode-character spans, plain runtime data, compiled JSON,
     generated plan, normalized emitted-source reconstruction, user-function transport, and semantic codeblock
     shape without a closure or body execution. Dart dynamic invocation `.11.5.2` now adds static-precedence bound
     fallback, once-only ordered arguments, copied/restored fixed/rest bindings, caller-visible nonparameter stores,
     local results and result access/chaining, exact typed failures/ordered recursion, and identical native,
     reconstructed, generated-plan, and independently compiled emitted-source behavior. Generic final-block
     equivalence `.11.5.3` now preserves exact final-only declaration/staged/registry/descriptor-v3 metadata and
     applies one signature-governed normalization to equivalent attached/parenthesized helper, typed-user-function,
     receiver, and tree blocks. All routes share the existing dynamic executor; eager/control/harray/explicit
     literal surfaces remain distinct. Complete Dart and canonical signoff closes parent `.11.5`. Julia
     construction `.11.6.1` now preserves the same inert eight-field literal, nullable fixed/final-rest signature,
     typed deferred body, containing Unicode-character spans, compiled/generated/emitted identity, user-function
     transport, and semantic codeblock shape without execution or capture. Focused 239/239, complete Julia,
     Knowledge Map, mdBook, doctrine, and canonical signoff are green; the leaf lands clean at `3a38338d`.
     Dynamic invocation `.11.6.2` now implements static-precedence bound fallback, once-only ordered arguments,
     copied/restored fixed/rest bindings, live caller nonparameter state, local results/access/chaining/discard,
     exact failures/ordered recursion, and native/reconstructed/generated/emitted identity. Focused 125/125 plus
     construction 239/239, variadic 55/55, complete package, and Julia-local gates are green. Knowledge Map
     770/6,251, mdBook 79/13,972 KiB, all seven doctrines, canonical containment/moved-root, CLI 66x2, RAM 57%,
     and Phase 0 1,031/1,031 in 655 seconds complete signoff; the leaf lands clean at `bc85c0fa`. Generic
     contextual final-block equivalence `.11.6.3` now preserves final-only typed signature metadata and applies
     one governed attached/parenthesized normalization across native/reconstructed/generated/emitted execution.
     Focused 125/125 dynamic + 118/118 contextual + 239/239 construction, the complete Julia-local gate, Knowledge
     Map 771/6,256, mdBook 79/13,980 KiB, all seven doctrines, canonical containment/moved-root, CLI 66x2, RAM 61%,
     and Phase 0 1,031/1,031 in 966 seconds complete signoff. Parent `.11.6` is closed. Behavior-free `.11.7.0`
     proves the unchanged neutral contract plus focused Perl/Rust/Dart/Julia consumers, identifies the missing
     composed recurring driver and stale mixed future exclusion, and measures Lua's exact parser/AST/call-dispatch
     gap. Historical four-implementation recurring/public governance is split as `.11.7.1-.2`; Lua typed construction, dynamic
     invocation, existing-emitter dual-ABI identity, and final admission are dependency-owned by `.11.8.0-.4`.
     Completed behavior-free `.11.8.0` freezes one `lua/test/callable_codeblock_literal_contract_test.lua` consumer and exact
     seams: `ActionCallableSignature`/eight-field inert state `.1`; colon keyword plus evaluated-value access and
     post-static scoped bound dispatch `.2`; native/reconstructed/generated/byte-fresh emitted identity `.3`; and
     replacement five-backend/dual-ABI recurring/public admission `.4`. No behavior or capability status changes
     in the planning slice. `.11.8.1` now implements that inert state on PUC Lua and LuaJIT. Its 168-assertion
     consumer proves exact fixed/rest records, Unicode/nested spans, all nine malformed codes, deferred scans,
     preserved containing spans across copies, user-function transport, compiled/generated/emitted effective
     state, and semantic signatures; the complete Lua gate remains green. `.11.8.2` now adds post-static bound
     invocation, colon-keyword rejection, evaluated-value access, dynamic caller frames, local results, exact
     failures, and ordered recursion at focused 232x2 plus complete dual-ABI Lua. `.11.8.3` now proves native,
     effective-`SpecFile` reconstruction, generated-plan, and independently loaded emitted-module identity through
     the same executor at focused 449x2 and registers exact Lua storage owner 17. Complete Lua, governance,
     Knowledge Map 779/6,322, sole-facing mdBook 79/14,048 KiB, all doctrines, and canonical Phase 0 1,031/1,031
     in 809 seconds plus the callable matrix complete signoff.
     Five-backend callable recurring/public admission is complete under `.11.8.4`: the same consumer runs on PUC
     Lua and LuaJIT, only the satisfied callable
     exclusion is removed, census 80/0/0 is preserved, and `.11.8`/`.11` close.
     Uniform-binding selector retirement `.12.1` is complete. It removes spec-facing `array(name)` / `hash(name)`
     selector and mutation semantics before Rust codeblock parity or resumed Lua work. Corrected inventory `.12.1.0` counts 600 exact forms in 82 tracked specs and
     splits neutral contract `.1`, five backend enablement leaves, source migration, hard rejection, and no-drift.
     The contract is adopted; Perl `.12.1.2`, Rust `.12.1.3`, Dart `.12.1.4`, Julia `.12.1.5`, and Lua `.12.1.6`
     execute the bare typed-binding behavior. All five backends are enabled; source migration `.12.1.7.1-.3`
     removes all 600 file-backed selectors and all 1,356 positive embedded-source occurrences. Perl hard rejection
     `.12.1.8.1`, Rust `.12.1.8.2`, Dart `.12.1.8.3`, Julia `.12.1.8.4`, and Lua `.12.1.8.5` are complete with
     native/generated diagnostics and retained-constructor proof. Cross-variant `.12.1.8.6` now locks all five
     boundaries and zero runtime compatibility in canonical CI; `.12.1.9` admits the final public surface.
7. Treat documentation clarity as a product contract:
   - prioritize readability over cleverness,
   - remove ambiguity instead of hand-waving over it,
   - explain semantics plainly,
   - use representative examples generously,
   - use fuller worked examples for newly landed user-facing surfaces when terse snippets would undersell or obscure the real supported shape,
   - hold already-landed user-facing DSL methods to that same documentation bar too, backfilling semantics notes and extensive worked examples when an older surface is still thinly documented,
   - add cross-cutting cookbook guides when one module-focused reference would fragment a high-frequency user-facing surface too much,
   - and avoid obfuscation in both user-facing docs and architecture notes.

## Work Phases
## Phase 0: Safety Net and Baseline Lock
- Build regression harness for all `specs/*.spec`.
- Add smoke tests for shipped core parser entry points such as `LibReader`, `TableGrep`, and
  `PPlugin`; retired non-core/plugin owners stay outside the core phase0 gate unless explicitly
  reintroduced under a new owner.
- Freeze baseline AST shapes for representative inputs.
- Exit criteria:
  - Green baseline suite (`PERL5LIB= prove -Iperl t/phase0_regression.t`, currently `PASS 1..1031`).
  - Known failures documented; no shipped-spec compile blocker is currently deferred.

## Phase 1: Parser-Core Isolation
- Reduce non-essential module coupling during LinkedSpec load/compile.
- Separate parser-core concerns from framework/global configuration concerns.
- Exit criteria:
  - LinkedSpec core can compile/run specs with minimal dependency surface.

## Phase 1A: LinkedSpec.pm Modularization (New Priority)
- Keep `perl/LinkedSpec.pm` as a thin orchestration façade with stable public APIs (`get_parser`, `Get`, compatibility helpers).
- Extract cohesive internal concerns into focused submodules/packages with explicit interfaces and behavior parity.
- Module boundaries (target structure):
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/Trace.pm`
    - Trace config + emission/routing (`configure_trace`, `log_output`, `log_dump`, `trace_enter`, `trace_exit`, `trace_decision`).
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/Validation.pm`
    - Spec and rule/dependency-regex validation (`validate_spec_content`, `validate_dsl_syntax`, `validate_rule_definition`, `validate_dependency_regex_references`).
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/Resolver.pm`
    - Spec path resolution + source loading (`_resolve_local_spec_path`, fallback rules, file-open error surfaces).
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/RuleIR.pm`
    - Rule IR collection/planning/validation/emit-context assembly.
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/ActionRewriter.pm` (historical plan — later removed)
    - Helper-contract catalog + canonical action-IR scanning/lowering.
    - Note: this module was extracted as planned, then collapsed to a thin forwarding shim over `RuleIR::EmitContext` and deleted entirely in Phase 1 (`PHASE1-PARSER-CORE-ISOLATION.2`). The focused helper-rewrite entrypoint now lives in `LinkedSpec::RuleIR::EmitContext::rewrite_action_code_for_compat(...)`. The remaining `RuleIR/ActionRewriter/Compiler` rollout-order mention below is likewise historical planning.
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/Compiler.pm`
    - `Get` compile pipeline orchestration.
  - `perl/LinkedSpec/BootstrapSpec.pm` (later slice)
    - Bootstrap grammar descriptor + bootstrap parse handlers.
- Shared-state policy:
  - Introduce/expand a shared context object (hashref) for cross-cutting state (`top_rule`, trace/runtime config, compile metadata) to reduce package-global coupling during extraction.
- Rollout policy:
  - Incremental, no-behavior-change slices.
  - Keep `t/phase0_regression.t` green after every slice.
  - Start with lowest-risk extractions first: **Trace -> Validation -> Resolver**, then proceed to RuleIR/ActionRewriter/Compiler and finally BootstrapSpec.
- Exit criteria:
  - `LinkedSpec.pm` delegates most internal work to extracted modules.
  - Existing behavior and regression baseline preserved.
  - Public API compatibility maintained.
- Recent landed follow-up: `LinkedSpec.pm` now centralizes the flat key/value option-pair normalization used by both `Get(...)` and `get_parser(...)` behind one tiny façade helper instead of duplicating the same even-pair / odd-trailing-fallback logic in both public wrappers. This is a no-behavior-change cleanup, but it keeps the remaining façade surface smaller and more uniform.
- Recent landed follow-up: `LinkedSpec::RuleIR::EmitContext` now centralizes its internal ActionIR owner-package registry plus `default_deps_for_package(...)` lookup through shared local helpers instead of repeating that package/dependency contract across dozens of local wrappers. This is still a no-behavior-change Backbone Item 3 cleanup slice, but it keeps the RuleIR-to-ActionIR bridge easier to audit as the lowering surface keeps growing.
- Recent landed follow-up: the core ActionIR lowering owners `FlowExpr`, `ValueExpr`, `MethodLowering`, and `DeclareMethod` now assemble their `default_deps_for_package(...)` callback maps through one shared `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch::build_dep_map(...)` helper instead of each owner hand-building another parallel callback registry. This is still a no-behavior-change Backbone Item 3 cleanup slice, but it keeps the active lowering contracts more uniform as the semantic surface grows.
- Recent landed follow-up: the remaining secondary ActionIR owners `ArrayPipeline`, `ControlFlow`, `Contracts`, `RewritePipeline`, `Diagnostics`, `StatementSplit`, `CanonicalEvents`, and `Scanner` now assemble their `default_deps_for_package(...)` callback maps through that same shared `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch::build_dep_map(...)` helper too, so default callback-map assembly is now broadly centralized across the active ActionIR owner surface instead of split between one shared seam and many owner-local inline registries.
- Recent landed follow-up: the dep-map-only ActionIR owners no longer keep unused local `_require_pkg(...)`, `_call_preserving_err(...)`, or `_require_pkg_cb(...)` wrappers after moving to `OwnerDispatch::build_dep_map(...)`; `ActionIR::Scanner` keeps its callback-loader wrapper because it still directly resolves `ScannerCore`.
- Recent landed follow-up: `ParserFactory.pm` no longer hand-builds its mixed trace/resolve/compile callback plus trace dump-level value bundle locally; `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch` now also owns a shared mixed dependency-bundle builder for that active compile-path seam, and focused regression coverage locks both the helper contract and the parser-factory source-level spend.
- Recent landed follow-up: `PluginBridge.pm` no longer hand-builds its registry-first / legacy-`PPlugin` fallback dependency callback map locally; its default registered-plugin lookup, legacy runtime loading, legacy lookup, and legacy exec callbacks now assemble through `LinkedSpec::OwnerDispatch::build_dep_map(...)` too.
- Recent landed follow-up: the remaining thin wrapper callback lookups in `Runtime`, `Compiler`, `BootstrapSpec`, `SpecEntry`, `ActionIR::Scanner`, and now `RuleIR::EmitContext`'s ActionIR owner dispatch route through `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg_cb(...)` instead of local `Package->can(...)` probes, leaving direct callback probing reserved for `OwnerDispatch` itself.
- Recent landed follow-up: `OwnerDispatch::dispatch_owner_call(...)` now resolves delegated owner callbacks through that same `require_pkg_cb(...)` loader path instead of carrying a second package-`can(...)` plus direct symbol-call route inside the shared dispatcher itself.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Runtime.pm`, `BootstrapSpec.pm`, and `ActionIR::Scanner.pm` no longer keep dead local `_require_pkg(...)` wrappers now that their live callback-loader paths already spend `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg_cb(...)`, and `RuleIR::EmitContext.pm` no longer keeps a dead local `_require_trace_pkg(...)` wrapper now that Trace access is already part of the ActionIR owner registry.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Trace.pm`, `Validation.pm`, `ActionIR::CanonicalEvents.pm`, and `ActionIR::StatementSplit.pm` no longer keep single-use local `OwnerDispatch` wrappers either; their live helper bodies now call `OwnerDispatch` inline where that was already the only real consumer.
- Recent landed follow-up: `ActionIR::StatementSplit.pm` no longer keeps a single-use `_require_statement_split_core_pkg(...)` loader wrapper either; `_split_action_ir_statements(...)` now lazy-loads `StatementSplit::Core` directly through `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg(...)` inside its live `$@`-preserving delegation body.
- Recent landed follow-up: `ActionIR::CanonicalEvents.pm` no longer keeps a single-use `_require_canonical_events_core_pkg(...)` loader wrapper either; `_canonicalize_helper_action_ir_event(...)` now lazy-loads `CanonicalEvents::Core` directly through `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg(...)` inside its live `$@`-preserving delegation body.
- Recent landed follow-up: `OwnerDispatch` lazy package loading is now anchored to an absolute repo `perl` root at module load time, so later `chdir(...)` no longer strands `get_parser(...)` and other lazy owner paths on stale relative `@INC` entries when they need to load `ParserFactory`, `RuntimeContext`, or deeper compile/runtime owners on demand.
- Recent landed follow-up: `BootstrapSpec.pm` no longer keeps a separate local `_require_bootstrap_core_pkg(...)` loader wrapper either; its meaningful `build_bootstrap_spec(...)` seam now resolves `BootstrapSpec::Core::build_bootstrap_spec(...)` directly through `OwnerDispatch::require_pkg_cb(...)` inside its `$@`-preserving body.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Runtime.pm`, `BootstrapSpec.pm`, and `ParserFactory.pm` no longer keep one-shot local `OwnerDispatch` callback-loader or `$@`-preservation wrappers either; their live orchestration bodies now spend `OwnerDispatch` directly for the compiler/bootstrap callback and successful `$@`-preservation seams.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Runtime.pm` no longer keeps a one-shot `_runtime_owner_handler_source_label(...)` wrapper either; runtime-owner fallback diagnostics call the shared runtime-context top-rule label helper directly.

## Phase 2: DSL Frontend Hardening
- Replace permissive/spec-skipping behavior with explicit token handling.
- Provide high-quality errors with line/column context.
- Keep a compatibility mode for legacy permissive behavior where needed.
- Phase 2 has now started with stricter rule-paragraph regex-token validation across both multiline and same-line rule bodies, and now also includes earlier rejection of malformed glued worded rule-mode suffixes such as `rule:ORX` / `rule::ANDX`, malformed glued edge-target suffixes such as `-> Rule-extra` / `=> Rule-extra`, malformed action-edge fluent starts such as `-> Rule.` / `-> Rule..push(...)`, malformed blind-call fluent starts such as `=> Rule.` / `=> Rule..return_a()`, stray preamble text before the first rule paragraph, unsupported top-level garbage lines inside a rule paragraph, unsupported same-line rule-header filler after the rule start or leading regex cluster, malformed `@...` split-marker spellings, top-level-only rule-start detection inside open code blocks, stray unmatched top-level closing delimiters like `}` / `)` / `]` inside rule paragraphs, unclosed open blocks at EOF, and early grouped-action-edge validation for shared-block forms like `-> RuleA | RuleB { ... }`, while blind-call fluent post-call continuations like `=> Rule .method(...)` are now part of the explicit supported surface.
- Exit criteria:
  - Deterministic DSL validation.
  - No silent token-loss in strict mode.

## Phase 3: Execution Semantics Clarification
- Introduce explicit parse modes:
  - `seek` mode (progressive extraction; can skip to anchors).
  - `consume` mode (contiguous matching discipline).
- Keep default behavior backward compatible.
- Historical first slice (superseded by ADR `0044` and the `.9.1.3-.9` rule-local rollout):
  - public `parse_mode => 'seek' | 'consume'` was supported through `LinkedSpec::Get(...)`, `LinkedSpec::get_parser(...)`, and `return_descriptor => 1`,
  - the default remains backward-compatible `seek`,
  - `consume` now drives contiguous `LinkedRE::or(...)` matching from the current input position,
  - generated descriptors exposed the selected mode at `meta->{parse_mode}`,
- Recent landed follow-up: the public descriptor-introspection option is now `return_descriptor => 1` with no active `return_descr` alias preserved, so the active runtime/parser-factory/compiler path and the current docs all use one explicit descriptor-return term.
  - the documented contract now states explicitly that `seek` / `consume` is cursor discipline while `OR` / `AND` is rule composition, so those axes should stay orthogonal as Phase 3 continues,
  - and the Phase 3 semantics notes now also state explicitly that current LinkedSpec is not targeting full parser-engine backtracking: `BACKTRACK()` / `IBACKTRACK()` are local cursor-rewind helpers inside a mostly forward-moving model, and well-written `.spec` files should not depend on systemic search-tree rollback.
- Exit criteria:
  - Clear documented behavior contract for each mode.

## Phase 4: Capture/Mark API Formalization
- Make position/capture concepts first-class and transparent:
  - Named marks/checkpoints.
  - Capture helpers based on marks and current match boundaries.
- Preserve `$CAPTURE` compatibility.
- Landed first slice:
  - named split/checkpoint marker `@mark(name)` is now supported alongside preferred anonymous marker `@capture_slice`, with `@capture_from_here` and `@move_pos` retained as compatibility aliases,
  - explicit helper `capture_slice()` now reads the current anonymous capture-boundary span directly without requiring a named checkpoint first,
  - explicit helper `capture_slice_len()` now reads the numeric width of that same current anonymous capture-boundary span without materializing the substring, with `capture_slice_length()` and older `capture_from_rule_start()` / `capture_len_from_rule_start()` retained as compatibility aliases,
  - explicit helpers `capture_slice_until_cursor()` / `capture_slice_until_cursor_len()` now read that same current anonymous capture boundary through the live parser cursor instead of stopping at the current match edge or extending through end-of-input,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_until_cursor()` now returns that same anonymous through-cursor span and then advances the anonymous capture boundary to the live parser cursor for rolling same-rule through-cursor flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_until_cursor_len()` now returns the width of that same anonymous through-cursor span and then advances the anonymous capture boundary to the live parser cursor for rolling same-rule through-cursor width flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_len()` now returns the width of that same anonymous current-edge span and then advances the anonymous capture boundary to the current parser position for rolling same-rule current-edge width flows,
  - explicit helpers `capture_take_rest()` / `capture_take_rest_len()` now return the same anonymous capture-boundary tail through end-of-input and then advance that anonymous boundary to end-of-input for rolling same-rule tail flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_slice_pos()` now reads the numeric position of that same current anonymous capture boundary directly without falling back to raw `$IPOS`,
  - explicit helper `capture_slice_line()` now reads the 1-based line number of that same current anonymous capture boundary for later diagnostics without counting newlines from `$IPOS` by hand,
  - explicit helper `capture_slice_col()` now reads the 1-based column number of that same current anonymous capture boundary for later diagnostics without spelling raw `$IPOS`-plus-newline math inline,
  - explicit helper `start_capture_slice()` now begins a new anonymous capture slice from inside lifecycle/action code without spelling `assign(s(IPOS), cursor_pos())`, with `capture_slice_here()` retained as a compatibility alias,
  - explicit helpers `capture_rest()` / `capture_rest_len()` now read the current anonymous capture-boundary tail through end-of-input, with `capture_rest_length()` retained as a compatibility alias,
  - live delimiter-reader bands in `specs/tkgui.spec`, `specs/hlink_substitution.spec`, and `specs/simenv.spec` now also spend `capture_slice()` directly instead of spelling raw anonymous-boundary `substr($$STRING, $IPOS, $LSPOS - $IPOS - ...)` reads by hand,
  - compatibility helper `capture_from(name)` now captures from that named checkpoint to the left edge of the current match,
  - explicit helper `capture_len_from(name)` now reads the numeric width of that same current-edge span without materializing the substring,
  - explicit helpers `capture_until_cursor_from(name)` / `capture_until_cursor_len_from(name)` now read that same named checkpoint through the live parser cursor instead of stopping at the current match edge or extending through end-of-input,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_until_cursor_from(name)` now returns that same named-mark through-cursor span and then advances the named checkpoint to the live parser cursor for rolling same-rule through-cursor flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_until_cursor_len_from(name)` now returns the width of that same named-mark through-cursor span and then advances the named checkpoint to the live parser cursor for rolling same-rule through-cursor width flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_len_from(name)` now returns the width of that same named-mark current-edge span and then advances the named checkpoint to the current parser position for rolling same-rule current-edge width flows,
  - explicit helpers `capture_take_rest_from(name)` / `capture_take_rest_len_from(name)` now return that same named-mark tail through end-of-input and then advance the named checkpoint to end-of-input for rolling same-rule tail flows,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_between_len(start_mark, end_mark)` now returns the width of that same explicit two-mark span and then advances the start mark to the stored end mark for rolling explicit-boundary width flows,
  - explicit helpers `mark_input_start(name)` / `mark_input_end(name)` now let a later action block snapshot the absolute current-input start and end boundaries into stable named checkpoints without forcing raw `0` / `length($$STRING)` plumbing, and they now lower as safe standalone writer statements even when the stored boundary itself is `0`,
  - explicit helper `capture_take(name)` now returns that same span and then advances the named checkpoint to the current parser position for rolling same-rule capture flows,
  - explicit helpers `capture_rest_from(name)` / `capture_rest_len_from(name)` now read the same named checkpoint through end-of-input without forcing that shape back through raw `mark_pos(...)` plus `substr(...)`,
  - explicit helper `capture_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now lets a later action block read the span between two explicit rule-local named checkpoints without depending on the current match edge,
  - explicit helper `capture_len_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now lets a later action block read the numeric width of that same explicit two-mark span without materializing the substring,
  - explicit helper `capture_take_between(start_mark, end_mark)` now returns that same explicit two-mark span and then advances the start mark to the stored end mark for rolling remembered-boundary flows,
  - explicit helper `mark_here(name)` now lets a later action block move or initialize that rule-local named checkpoint without bundling the write into the read,
  - explicit helpers `mark_entry_start(name)` / `mark_entry_end(name)` now let a later action block snapshot the immediate entry match boundaries into stable named checkpoints without forcing raw `entry_*_pos()` plus `mark_pos(...)` plumbing,
  - explicit helper `mark_match_start(name)` now lets a later action block store the left edge of the current match instead of the post-match parser position,
  - explicit helper `mark_match_end(name)` now lets a later action block store the right edge of the current local match as a stable named checkpoint even if the live parser cursor later moves elsewhere,
  - explicit helper `mark_copy(target_mark, source_mark)` now lets a later action block copy one explicit remembered boundary into another named checkpoint and clear the target if the source is absent,
  - explicit helper `clear_mark(name)` now lets a later action block drop that rule-local named checkpoint without overloading mutation into the read helpers,
  - explicit helper `mark_exists(name)` now lets a later action block ask whether that rule-local named checkpoint is currently present without reading or mutating it,
  - explicit helper `mark_pos(name)` now lets a later action block read the stored numeric position of that rule-local named checkpoint without mutating it,
  - explicit helper `mark_line(name)` now lets a later action block read the 1-based line number of that stored named checkpoint without mutating it,
  - explicit helper `mark_col(name)` now lets a later action block read the 1-based column number of that stored named checkpoint without mutating it,
  - explicit helper `cursor_col()` now lets a later action block read the current parser cursor column directly without spelling raw same-line position math inline,
  - explicit helper `cursor_line()` now lets a later action block read the current parser cursor line directly without spelling raw newline counting inline,
  - explicit helper `input_text()` now lets a later action block read the whole current input string directly without falling back to raw `$$STRING` or first storing absolute input-boundary marks,
  - explicit helper `input_slice(start, width)` now lets a later action block read a whole-input substring from already-known absolute source boundaries without falling back to raw `substr($$STRING, ...)`,
  - explicit helper `input_len()` now lets a later action block read the width of that same whole current input directly without falling back to raw `length($$STRING)` or first storing absolute input-boundary marks,
  - explicit helper `input_end_pos()` now lets a later action block read the absolute position of the whole-input right edge directly without first storing an absolute input-end mark or overloading width-oriented `input_len()` wording for boundary-oriented logic,
  - explicit helpers `input_end_line()` / `input_end_col()` now let a later action block read the human-readable line/column of the whole-input right edge directly without first storing absolute input-end marks or spelling manual end-of-input line/column math inline,
  - structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` payloads on `compiler_pipeline:build_compiled_rule_table`, `compiler_pipeline:build_final_descriptor`, and attributed `compiler_pipeline:validate_dependency_regex_references` failures now also preserve a synthetic `handler_source_label` like `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<rule_label>` whenever the failing rule is already known even if no exact handler variant has been selected yet,
  - structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` payloads on rule-attributed `compiler_pipeline:validate_dsl_syntax` false-return failures now also preserve the validator’s specific summary/detail plus the same synthetic `handler_source_label` whenever the offending rule paragraph is already known,
  - structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` payloads on `compiler_pipeline:validate_spec_content` false-return failures now also preserve the validator’s specific summary/detail, and malformed-first-rule failures now also preserve the same synthetic `handler_source_label` whenever that early rule label is already known,
  - parser-factory `validate_spec_name` false-return failures now also preserve the validator’s own summary/detail instead of collapsing to a generic “validate_spec_name rejected the requested parser name” wrapper,
  - parser-factory `resolve_spec_path` false-return failures from the real resolver now also preserve the resolver’s own summary/detail such as `Spec path not found` or `Spec path is not a file` instead of collapsing to a generic “Spec resolution failed” wrapper,
  - parser-factory `load_spec_content` false-return failures from the real resolver now also preserve the resolver’s own summary/detail such as `Unable to open spec file '...'` plus `OS Error: ...` instead of collapsing to a generic “Spec file load failed” wrapper,
  - parser-factory `compile_spec` malformed defined return shapes now also preserve specific parser-result detail such as `compile_spec returned invalid parser value: HASH; expected CODE` instead of accepting arbitrary defined values as successful parser results or collapsing back to one generic compile wrapper string,
  - parser-factory and runtime mode-only compile branches now also preserve explicit malformed result detail such as `compile_spec returned invalid parse_only value: HASH; expected undef` or `run_get_pipeline returned invalid generate_only value: ARRAY; expected undef` instead of letting defined values masquerade as successful `parse_only` / `generate_only` runs,
  - runtime `run_get_pipeline` malformed defined return shapes now also preserve specific mode-aware result detail such as `run_get_pipeline returned invalid parser value: HASH; expected CODE` or `run_get_pipeline returned invalid descriptor value: ARRAY; expected HASH` instead of accepting arbitrary defined values as successful inline compile results,
  - runtime-owner fallback `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` payloads now also preserve the same label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the selected `top_rule` is already known at that seam,
  - compiler `bootstrap_parse` malformed non-throwing return shapes now also preserve specific shape detail such as `bootstrap_parse returned undef while reporting parse_success=1` or `bootstrap_parse returned HASH while reporting parse_success=1; expected ARRAY` instead of collapsing to one generic invalid-IR detail string,
  - compiler `build_compiled_rule_table` malformed non-throwing `compile_spec_entry(...)` return shapes now also preserve specific tuple detail such as `compile_spec_entry returned invalid descriptor tuple: label=undef, info=undef` or `compile_spec_entry returned invalid descriptor tuple: label='Top', info=ARRAY` instead of collapsing to one generic descriptor-build detail string,
  - top-level `runtime_parser:resolve_top_rule_handler` failures now also preserve that same label-scoped synthetic `handler_source_label` whenever the selected top-rule label is known, even if the compiled descriptor entry for that rule is missing before any exact handler variant can be discovered,
  - explicit helper `entry_text()` now lets a later action block read the current immediate match text directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - generalized `return(payload)` and fluent `.return(payload)` now also recognize immediate text/group helper payloads like `entry_text()` directly, so documented forms such as `return(entry_text())` and live-spec forms such as `I.return(entry_text())` stay on the canonical helper path instead of falling back to raw Perl or legacy label-injected return rendering,
  - explicit helper `entry_line()` now lets a later action block read the current immediate match line directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_start_line()` now lets a later action block read that same immediate-match left-edge line with fully explicit start-edge naming when that reads more clearly alongside `entry_start_pos()` and `entry_end_*()`,
  - explicit helper `entry_col()` now lets a later action block read the current immediate match column directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_start_col()` now lets a later action block read that same immediate-match left-edge column with fully explicit start-edge naming when that reads more clearly alongside `entry_start_pos()` and `entry_end_*()`,
  - explicit helper `entry_groups()` now lets a later action block snapshot the whole current immediate-match capture-group list directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_named(name)` now lets a later action block read one current immediate-match named capture directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_has(name)` now lets a later action block ask whether one current immediate-match named capture is present without first snapshotting the whole named-capture hash,
  - explicit helper `entry_map()` now lets a later action block snapshot the whole current immediate-match named-capture hash directly without first storing a named checkpoint, with `entry_named_map()` retained as a compatibility alias,
  - explicit helper `entry_len()` now lets a later action block read the width of the current immediate match directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_start_pos()` now lets a later action block read the left edge of the current immediate match directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `entry_end_pos()` now lets a later action block read the right edge of the current immediate match directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helpers `entry_end_line()` / `entry_end_col()` now let a later action block read the human-readable line/column of that same immediate-match right edge directly without manual newline or same-line arithmetic around `entry_end_pos()`,
  - explicit helper `match_text()` now lets a later action block read the current local match text directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_line()` now lets a later action block read the current local match line directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_start_line()` now lets a later action block read that same local-match left-edge line with fully explicit start-edge naming when that reads more clearly alongside `match_start_pos()` and `match_end_*()`,
  - explicit helper `match_col()` now lets a later action block read the current local match column directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_start_col()` now lets a later action block read that same local-match left-edge column with fully explicit start-edge naming when that reads more clearly alongside `match_start_pos()` and `match_end_*()`,
  - explicit helper `match_groups()` now lets a later action block snapshot the whole current local-match capture-group list directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_named(name)` now lets a later action block read one current local-match named capture directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_has(name)` now lets a later action block ask whether one current local-match named capture is present without first snapshotting the whole named-capture hash,
  - explicit helper `match_map()` now lets a later action block snapshot the whole current local-match named-capture hash directly without first storing a named checkpoint, with `match_named_map()` retained as a compatibility alias,
  - explicit helper `match_start_pos()` now lets a later action block read the left edge of the current local match directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helper `match_end_pos()` now lets a later action block read the right edge of the current local match directly without first storing a named checkpoint,
  - explicit helpers `match_end_line()` / `match_end_col()` now let a later action block read the human-readable line/column of that same local-match right edge directly without manual newline or same-line arithmetic around `match_end_pos()`,
  - core token readers in `specs/ebnf.spec` now also use the explicit immediate-match helper surface directly (`entry_text()` / `entry_group(0)` plus helper-method cleanup) instead of raw `$IMATCH`-style reads for that simple terminal-reader band,
  - the debug-print token-reader rules in `specs/BNF.spec` now also use `entry_text()` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` surface for those simple token reads,
  - the debug-print token-reader rules in `specs/DT.spec` now also use `entry_text()` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` surface for those simple token reads,
  - the debug-print token-reader rules in `specs/operators_try.spec` now also use `entry_text()` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` surface for those simple token reads,
  - the top `comment` and `space` token readers in `specs/vhdl.spec` now also use `entry_text()` directly instead of raw `$IMATCH`,
  - `specs/vhdl.spec::concurrent_signal_assignment_statement` now also uses explicit reordered `entry_group(1)` / `entry_group(2)` / `entry_group(0)` reads instead of raw `@IMATCH_LIST[-2, -1, 0]`,
  - the small declaration-reader band in `specs/vhdl.spec::{constant_declaration,variable_declaration,file_declaration,signal_declaration,configuration_specification}` now also uses explicit `declare(...=entry_group(...))` locals instead of raw `@IMATCH_LIST` destructuring,
  - `specs/vhdl.spec::interface_signal_declaration` now also uses an explicit helper array seeded from `entry_groups()` and `array_copy(...)` for its `?port_decl:` payload instead of mutating raw `@IMATCH_LIST` with `push @IMATCH_LIST, call(signal_decl_range)`,
  - the package-name reads in `specs/vhdl.spec::package_declaration[1]` and `specs/vhdl.spec::package_body[1]` now also use `entry_group(0)` directly instead of `scalar(IMATCH_LIST, 0)`,
  - the helper-style whole-group-list returns in `specs/vhdl.spec::subprogram_declaration`, `subprogram_body`, `type_declaration`, and `process_statement` now also use `entry_groups()` instead of `IMATCH_LIST`,
  - the compact token-reader band in `specs/Lispish.spec` now also uses `entry_text()` / `entry_group(0)` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` / `scalar(IMATCH_LIST, 0)` surface for those simple token payload readers,
  - `specs/tkgui.spec::sub_gui` now also uses `entry_group(0)` directly instead of raw `@IMATCH_LIST` destructuring for its simple entry-point name read,
  - the `anyvariable`, `variable_substitution`, and `comments` token-reader rules in `specs/simenv.spec` now also use `entry_text()` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` surface for those simple token reads,
  - grouped attribute readers in `specs/lib_reader.spec` now also use `entry_group(...)` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH_LIST, ...)` surface for those simple grouped entry captures,
  - the terminal token band in `specs/tablegrep.spec` now also uses `entry_group(0..2)` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH_LIST, ...)` surface for those simple regex-token capture reads,
  - `specs/portmap.spec::bare_bit_slice` now also uses `entry_text()` / `entry_group(...)` / `entry_groups()` directly instead of the older `scalar(IMATCH)` / `scalar(IMATCH_LIST, ...)` / `flat_array(IMATCH_LIST)` surface for that live classification rule,
  - concise container aliases `s(...)` / `a(...)` / `h(...)` are now supported as exact short spellings for `scalar(...)` / `array(...)` / `hash(...)` without introducing Perl-sigil syntax,
  - the core method-DSL band in `specs/ebnf.spec` now also spends those concise aliases on real grammar orchestration and terminal readers, preferring `s(...)` / `a(...)` over the longer `scalar(...)` / `array(...)` spellings in that migrated band,
  - the grouped reader band in `specs/lib_reader.spec` now also spends those concise aliases on real grouped cleanup, split, and return payload paths,
  - the dense `parenthesis` orchestration plus token-reader band in `specs/Lispish.spec` now also spends those concise aliases on real working-state flow and typed payload returns,
  - `specs/portmap.spec::bare_bit_slice` now also spends the concise array alias on its group snapshot and classification return payloads,
  - the `LE` aggregation line in `specs/pplugin.spec::pplugin_top` now also spends the concise array alias on its live subdef accumulator path,
  - the split/accumulation band in `specs/sdce.spec` now also spends those concise aliases on cursor state, direct cursor-position reads, anonymous capture-boundary reads via `capture_slice()`, accumulator pushes, split targets/sources, and return payloads,
  - `specs/sdce.spec::get_pinport` now also uses `entry_groups()` inside `flat_array(...)` instead of `IMATCH_LIST` in its helper return,
  - the `substitute_top` orchestration band in `specs/hlink_substitution.spec` now also spends the concise scalar/array aliases on call-result assignment, accumulator pushes, and aggregate return flow,
  - the main `vhistory` orchestration band in `specs/ds_vhistory.spec` now also spends those concise aliases on captured-entry reads, object-hierarchy accumulation, reset paths, and top-level return payload construction,
  - the simple token-return seams in `specs/regdef.spec` now also spend `entry_groups()` plus `flat_array(...)` instead of raw `@IMATCH_LIST` returns,
  - named marks are now scoped to the current rule label rather than shared across child-rule calls,
  - mark writes now fire only when the regex slot that actually carries the `@mark(name)` paragraph member matches,
  - high/debug trace output now shows mark-write positions for `@mark(name)`, `mark_here(name)`, `mark_match_start(name)`, and the advancing writes inside `capture_take(name)` and `capture_take_between(start_mark, end_mark)` as a short input excerpt plus a caret under the stored checkpoint position,
  - and the docs now spell out the rule-local scope, same-slot versus later-slot timing rule, stored-mark-versus-current-local-match-boundary reads, stable versus advancing versus explicit-two-mark versus explicit-write versus explicit-clear named-capture helpers, trace-visible checkpoint writes, and several worked examples.
- Recent landed follow-up:
  - explicit anonymous/named bridge helpers `start_capture_slice_from(name)` and `mark_capture_slice(name)` now let one rule move between the anonymous rolling-boundary model and the stable named-checkpoint model without raw `$IPOS` plus `mark_pos(...)` plumbing, and the docs now teach that bridge with direct worked examples,
  - the remaining obvious raw live-cursor and anonymous capture-boundary reads in `specs/vhdl.spec` now also spend `cursor_pos()`, `capture_slice()`, and `start_capture_slice()` directly instead of raw `pos $$STRING`, raw anonymous-boundary `substr(...)`, and direct `assign(scalar(IPOS), pos $$STRING)` in those migrated bands.
  - the lowercase header-return seams in `specs/vhdl.spec::{entity_declaration,architecture_body,component_instantiation_statement}` now also snapshot `entry_groups()`, normalize them with `lowercase_each(...)`, and rebuild the prior tagged/array-return payloads explicitly instead of using raw `map {lc} @IMATCH_LIST`.
- Exit criteria:
  - Cleaner author experience for staged extraction patterns.

## Phase 5: Runtime and Diagnostics Modernization
- Reduce runtime string `eval` frequency in hot parse paths.
- Improve debug tracing and rule-level instrumentation.
- Introduce consistent error objects/messages instead of abrupt process exits.
- Recent landed follow-up: parser-factory setup failures now normalize into `parser_factory:prepare_parser_factory` when the file-oriented owner cannot prepare its dependency/trace surface cleanly before normal validation/resolution begins.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiler-owner setup failures before the main validation/parse flow now normalize into `compiler_pipeline:prepare_pipeline` when a runtime context exists, instead of drifting outward as raw dies and relying on the generic runtime-owner fallback.
- Recent landed follow-up: `LinkedSpec::Runtime::run_get(...)` now also normalizes raw compiler-delegation dies and silent delegated `undef` returns into a structured fallback `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` payload when no deeper owner payload exists yet, while preserving richer deeper `last_error` payloads when they do.
- Recent landed follow-up: the inline runtime compile boundary now also treats only a real parser coderef as a successful default `run_get_pipeline(...)` result and only a descriptor hash as a successful `return_descriptor => 1` result, with malformed defined return shapes preserving specific detail instead of escaping as bogus success values.
- Recent landed follow-up: those same runtime and parser-factory compile boundaries now also lock the mode-only success contract explicitly, so `parse_only` / `generate_only` treat only `undef` as success and preserve specific malformed defined-result detail instead of silently accepting stray values.
- Recent landed follow-up: `runtime_owner:run_get_pipeline` fallback payloads now also preserve the label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known, so runtime-owner fallback diagnostics line up better with the rest of the structured handler-attribution story.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiler `prepare_pipeline` and `bootstrap_parse` failures now also preserve that same label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known, so early inline compile failures line up with the same structured handler-attribution story too.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiler `validate_spec_content` and unattributed `validate_dsl_syntax` failures now also preserve that same label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known, so pre-rule compiler validation failures no longer lose entrypoint attribution just because rule ownership has not been discovered yet.
- Recent landed follow-up: later compiler seams now follow the same fallback too, so unattributed `build_compiled_rule_table`, `build_final_descriptor`, and `validate_dependency_regex_references` failures also preserve `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known but no more specific rule attribution exists yet.
- Recent landed follow-up: the same late generic compiler-failure attribution is now regression-locked through the file-oriented `get_parser(...)` path too, so resolved-spec continuity keeps `spec_path`, selected `top_rule`, and `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` together even when a later generic compiler failure happens after file load.
- Recent landed follow-up: file-oriented runtime-parser failures are now regression-locked too, so after `get_parser(...)` has returned a parser, a later top-level parser invocation failure still keeps resolved `spec_path`, selected `top_rule`, and generated-handler identity together in shared `runtime_ctx->{last_error}`.
- Recent landed follow-up: returned parser coderefs now reject malformed non-`SCALAR`-reference input explicitly at `runtime_parser:validate_input_ref`, so top-level parser misuse preserves targeted structured detail and the same `top_rule` / handler-identity continuity instead of falling through to lower-level Perl dereference errors; the same file-oriented continuity is now regression-locked through `get_parser(...)` too.
- Recent landed follow-up: the low-level `build_compiled_rule_table(...)` surface now accepts `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx` too, and thrown `compile_spec_entry(...)` failures, malformed non-throwing descriptor tuples, invalid non-CODE callback contracts, malformed non-ARRAY parsed-entry containers, plus malformed individual parsed entries there now normalize into `compiler_pipeline:build_compiled_rule_table` payloads with `top_rule` / `rule_label` / `handler_source_label` continuity instead of leaving low-level callers with only a raw die or local undef path.
- Recent landed follow-up: the default `build_dependency_regex_map(...)` seam now also validates malformed descriptor shape explicitly, so `compiler_pipeline:build_final_descriptor` can preserve targeted detail like `build_dependency_regex_map expects rule 'Top' dependency_refs to be ARRAY ref; got SCALAR` with active `rule_label` / `handler_source_label` continuity instead of leaking incidental Perl reference failures from deep inside dependency-regex-map assembly.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Compiler.pm` now has one explicit internal compiled-spec state model. `build_compiled_rule_table(...)` first builds `kind/version/definition_order/compiled_rule_order/rules_by_label/redefined_rule_labels` state, default `build_dependency_regex_map(...)` now enriches that state directly, and final descriptor assembly now projects outward `spec` / `dependency_regex_map` hashes while exposing `meta.descriptor_model`, `meta.compiled_rule_order`, and `meta.redefined_rule_labels`. Low-level callers can also request the same state directly with `build_compiled_rule_table(..., { return_state => 1 })`.
- Recent landed follow-up: descriptor-level `action_rewriter_migration` summary generation is now state-first too. `Compiler.pm` now builds that summary directly from compiled-spec state instead of projecting through the legacy `spec` hash first, so non-priority summary rule lists such as `language_agnostic_ready_rules` and `compatibility_surface_ready_rules` now follow source rule order while the explicit `*_by_priority` views keep their separate blocker/compatibility triage ordering.
- Recent landed follow-up: final descriptor assembly now also has one explicit internal `compiled_descriptor_state` seam over compiled-spec state plus the compiled dependency-regex map, `return_descriptor` metadata now exposes `meta.definition_order`, and malformed compiled dependency-regex-map callback output is now rejected directly at that final descriptor boundary with a specific contract diagnostic.
- Recent landed follow-up: generated-descriptor validation now also consumes that same `compiled_descriptor_state` directly on the active path instead of flattening back to parallel `spec` and `dependency_regex_map` hashes first, so the compiler now stays on the explicit state model through validation and only projects the outward descriptor after that seam.
- Recent landed follow-up: that generated-descriptor validation seam is now fully state-first internally too. `Validation.pm` now validates descriptor state directly instead of bouncing back through the historical legacy `validate_dependency_regex_references(...)` entrypoint, so compatibility descriptor projection is fully deferred until descriptor-state validation has already succeeded.
- Recent landed follow-up: derived dependency regexes now also have one explicit internal state seam. `build_dependency_regex_map(...)` can now return `compiled_dependency_regex_state`, and final descriptor assembly now composes compiled-spec state plus compiled dependency-regex state instead of storing raw dependency-regex-map hashes as the last anonymous compiler-owned structure.
- Recent landed follow-up: that same derived compiler payload now uses the explicit dependency-regex name all the way through the active state model instead of keeping `compiled_gdata_state` / `gdata_by_label` / `gdata_rows` compatibility aliases inside `CompilerState`.
- Recent landed follow-up: the compiled state model now also has its own owner module. `LinkedSpec::CompilerState` now owns compiled-spec / compiled dependency-regex / compiled-descriptor state construction, shape checks, and legacy projection, so `Compiler.pm` and `Validation.pm` no longer carry duplicate local state-model logic.
- Recent landed follow-up: the last legacy compatibility-shape normalization rules for compiled spec and compiled dependency-regex maps now also route through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so `Compiler.pm` no longer keeps a second local “accept legacy hash or compiled-state record” conversion seam beside the state owner.
- Recent landed follow-up: read-side compiled-state access now also routes through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so compiler trace/logging reads and descriptor-validation rule-map reads no longer poke raw compiled-state fields directly from `Compiler.pm` or `Validation.pm`.
- Recent landed follow-up: ordered compiled-rule iteration now also routes through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so compiler consumers no longer rebuild the active rule stream ad hoc from `compiled_rule_order + rules_by_label` when walking compiled spec state.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiled-spec dependency lookup in `build_dependency_regex_map(...)` now also routes through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so compiler consumers no longer probe the compiled rule map directly just to check dependency presence or fetch one dependent rule record.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiled-descriptor metadata assembly now also routes through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so `Compiler.pm` no longer shapes descriptor metadata by mutating owner-provided compiled-spec metadata locally.
- Recent landed follow-up: descriptor migration-summary shaping now also routes through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so `Compiler.pm` no longer computes the large `action_rewriter_migration` reduction locally over compiled rules.
- Recent landed follow-up: descriptor-state validation now also walks owner-provided descriptor-state rule rows, dependency-regex rows, and by-label lookup through `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so `Validation.pm` no longer flattens descriptor state back into raw rule/dependency-regex maps on the active state-first path.
- Recent landed follow-up: descriptor-state validation now also consumes one owner-provided validation view from `LinkedSpec::CompilerState`, so `Validation.pm` no longer issues repeated owner lookups from inside its validation loops once the descriptor-state path is already chosen.
- Recent landed follow-up: `Validation.pm` now also uses one shared validation-view engine for both legacy `validate_dependency_regex_references(...)` inputs and owner-provided descriptor-state validation views, so the project no longer carries two separate copies of the same dependency-regex/rule consistency checks.
- Recent landed follow-up: `LinkedSpec::BootstrapSpec` and `BootstrapSpec::Core` now use `rule_descriptors` / `dispatch_state` naming for the bootstrap parser tuple and dispatch metadata instead of the remaining active `spec_descr` / `gdata` vocabulary, keeping bootstrap internals aligned with the compiler-state terminology cleanup without changing parse behavior.
- Recent landed follow-up: parser-factory pre-compile failure seams now also preserve that same label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known, so `prepare_parser_factory`, `validate_spec_name`, `resolve_spec_path`, and `load_spec_content` failures line up with the same structured handler-attribution story too.
- Recent landed follow-up: parser-factory `compile_spec` fallback payloads now also preserve that same label-only generated-handler identity `LinkedSpec::generated_handler:<top_rule>` whenever the requested top rule is already known, so file-oriented compile failures line up with the same structured handler-attribution story as runtime-owner and parser-boundary failures.
- Recent landed follow-up: `SpecEntry.pm` now compiles generated rule-handler source at most once per rule and reuses the compiled coderef across later invocations, instead of string-evaling the full handler on every call; invalid generated handler source now records explicit `runtime_handler:rule_handler_compile` failure context on first invocation.
- Recent landed follow-up: `SpecEntry.pm` now compiles that cached generated rule-handler source eagerly when the runtime handler wrapper is built, instead of waiting until the first rule invocation to string-eval the handler body; invocation-time failure reporting stays the same, but the runtime hot path no longer performs that handler-source compile step.
- Recent landed follow-up: invalid generated rule-handler source now keeps its compile warnings inside structured `runtime_handler:rule_handler_compile` diagnostics instead of leaking them to stderr during eager wrapper construction; regression coverage now locks the no-stderr behavior and the preserved warning/detail text in `last_error`.
- Recent landed follow-up: the returned parser now clears stale inner `runtime_handler` failure context after a successful top-level parse returns a defined AST, so `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` stays a true failure-only channel instead of preserving inner backtracking noise from a path that ultimately succeeded.
- Recent landed follow-up: structured `runtime_ctx->{last_error}` set/clear behavior now lives in a shared `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` owner used by `Runtime.pm`, `ParserFactory.pm`, `Compiler.pm`, and `SpecEntry.pm`, which removes duplicated payload-builder logic and makes the diagnostics contract harder to drift across owner modules.
- Recent landed follow-up: `runtime_ctx_ref` normalization/seeded-context materialization now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `Runtime.pm` and `ParserFactory.pm` no longer each carry their own copy of the SCALAR/HASH/shared-hash-slot coercion logic; lazy-load regression coverage now locks that `RuntimeContext` stays out of the public façade until `Get(...)` or `get_parser(...)` actually enters the runtime/parser-factory path.
- Recent landed follow-up: the reusable populated scalar-slot `runtime_ctx_ref => \$ctx` contract is now regression-locked through the public `LinkedSpec::Get(...)` and `LinkedSpec::get_parser(...)` entrypoints too, so facade-level callers can reuse the same context hashref across calls while preserving caller-owned fields and refreshing LinkedSpec-owned identity/error state.
- Recent landed follow-up: parser-source chunk allocation/configuration/emission and full capture reset now also live in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `Runtime.pm`, `Compiler.pm`, and `SpecEntry.pm` no longer each carry their own small parser-source callback/chunk-management helpers, and `run_get(...)`, `get_parser(...)`, plus low-level `build_compiled_rule_table(...)` preparation now share one reset helper where they need to drop stale emit callbacks; focused regression coverage now locks the shared helper behavior directly.
- Recent landed follow-up: `top_rule` reset/update, stale `spec_name` / `spec_path` identity reset, and resolved `spec_path` assignment now also live in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `Runtime.pm`, `ParserFactory.pm`, and `SpecEntry.pm` no longer hand-edit those runtime-context fields directly; focused regression coverage now locks the shared helper behavior for those state fields too.
- Recent landed follow-up: `run_get(...)` and `get_parser(...)` runtime-context preparation now clear stale `last_error` at the boundary too, matching the low-level `build_compiled_rule_table(...)` path so reused contexts do not preserve unrelated structured failures into the next operation.
- Recent landed follow-up: top-rule generated-handler source labels now also build through `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `Runtime.pm`, `ParserFactory.pm`, and compiler diagnostics share the same selected-`top_rule` attribution helper instead of each owner rebuilding that label locally; the compiler's parser-invocation boundary now also passes selected handler variants through that shared helper.
- Recent landed follow-up: compiler rule-or-top generated-handler source-label fallback now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so compiler diagnostics prefer a concrete rule label when known and share the same selected-`top_rule` fallback when attribution is still generic.
- Recent landed follow-up: rule-metadata generated-handler source labels now also build through `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `SpecEntry.pm` no longer extracts `selected_handler_variant` locally before constructing generated handler source labels.
- Recent landed follow-up: `SpecEntry.pm` no longer keeps a one-shot `_generated_handler_source_label(...)` wrapper either; runtime-handler construction calls the shared runtime-context rule-metadata label helper directly.
- Recent landed follow-up: the shared `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext` owner now also answers the “only write a fallback `last_error` when a deeper structured payload is absent” question, so `Runtime.pm` and `ParserFactory.pm` no longer each hand-roll that preservation check around compiler/parser-factory fallback failures; focused regression coverage now locks both the fresh-write and preserve-existing paths.
- Recent landed follow-up: owner-default `last_error` typing now also lives in `LinkedSpec::RuntimeContext`, so `Runtime.pm`, `ParserFactory.pm`, `Compiler.pm`, and `SpecEntry.pm` no longer each hand-apply their own fallback type (`runtime_owner`, `parser_factory`, `compiler_pipeline`, `runtime_handler`) before delegating into the shared error builder. - Recent landed follow-up: `ParserFactory.pm` now centralizes its small `RuntimeContext` wrapper surface behind one private owner-dispatch helper instead of repeating the same lazy-load and direct package-call pattern across `_prepare_runtime_ctx_for_get_parser(...)`, `_set_runtime_ctx_last_error(...)`, `_set_runtime_ctx_last_error_unless_present(...)`, and `_set_runtime_ctx_spec_path(...)`. Focused regression coverage now locks both the helper behavior and an end-to-end reused-context parser-source capture case.

Focused regression coverage now locks both the helper behavior and an end-to-end reused-context resolution-failure case.
